# Creature

*[Source](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/Creature) &middot; revision 319591 &middot; retrieved 2026-09-11 &middot; [wikitext](../../mirror/wikitext/Main/Creature.wiki)*

---
<table><tbody><tr><td><div><b>v53.16</b> · <a href="../DF2014/Creature.md">v0.47.05</a></div>This article is about the current version of DF.<br/>Note that some content may still need to be updated.<br/>
<div><a href="../Dwarf_Fortress_Wiki/V.md">More Info</a> · <a href="../Template/Av.md">V</a></div></td></tr>
</tbody></table>

[![Creatures preview2.png](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/d/d8/Creatures_preview2.png)](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Creatures_preview2.png)

In *Dwarf Fortress*, a **creature** is defined as any animate, normally-mobile (and for the sake of this article, non-[vermin](Vermin.md)) being that can interact with the world and any element inside it. The creatures in the game range from being entirely realistic to completely mythical. Although most creatures are animals, [dwarves](Dwarf.md), [giant cave spiders](Giant_cave_spider.md), and even [megabeasts](Megabeast.md) are all also considered creatures. Various creatures can and will interact with a fortress or adventurer in many different ways.

Some creatures have [skills](Skill.md) that match what type of creature they are (e.g. [monkeys](Monkey.md) having legendary climbing skill). Though most creatures can be found in any mode, some are exclusive to [adventure mode](Adventure_mode~2.md) or [fortress mode](Fortress_mode~2.md). Some creatures are randomly and procedurally generated, meaning they could have many different sprites in-game. Creatures of that type may have just one to a few sprites showcased out of many in the list below. A question-mark placeholder may also be shown instead. Also note that creatures with the `[ARENA_RESTRICTED]` or `[DOES_NOT_EXIST]` tokens cannot be spawned in the [object testing arena](Object_testing_arena.md), similarly to [vermin](Vermin.md) (e.g. [flies](Fly.md), [worms](Worm.md)).

Nearly all creatures in the game, including [very](Megabeast.md) [large](Giant_sperm_whale.md) [ones](Sea_serpent.md), take the space of a single tile, even if their [sprites](Graphics.md) imply otherwise ([wagons](Wagons.md) being the only exception). There are 870 creatures in the game. In adventure mode, creatures can have different labels to differentiate similar ones from [historical figures](Historical_figure.md). For example, a goblin may be labeled as a "white-haired goblin bowyer" while another would be a "high-cheekbones goblin bowyer".

There are also [extinct creatures](Extinction.md), which exist as a separate category, that can be completely removed from a world, existing either in the wild or only as domesticated beings, depending on the player's settings. These extinct creatures are based on real-world prehistoric ones, and also - like the other animals in the game - exist in humanoid form.

### Spawning

[![](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/4/44/Creatures_many.png)](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Creatures_many.png)

[https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Creatures_many.png](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Creatures_many.png)

Many creatures packed into one area, but in ASCII mode.

The creatures that will spawn on any given fortress map depend on the biome(s) that the fortress is in. Additionally, there are several [creature tokens](Creature_token~2.md) in the [raws](Raws.md) that deal with creature spawning:

- `[FREQUENCY:X]`: This tag dictates *how often* a creature will spawn. It ranges from 0-100, and is a comparative number, where the higher this number is, the more likely the creature is to spawn.
- `[CLUSTER_NUMBER:X]`: This determines *how many* creatures will appear at one time on a map.
- `[POPULATION_NUMBER:X]`: This determines the *total number* of this type of creature that can *ever* visit your fortress - the exact number varies, depending on the map.

For example, [deer](Deer.md) have a `[POPULATION_NUMBER:15:30]`, meaning that if you kill between 15-30 deer, no more deer will ever visit your fortress.

### Reading the Table

| Graphic | Tile | Name | Playable | Hostile | Food Source | Adult Body Size | Pet Value (×) | Biome | Features |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

The above columns indicate, in order:

- **Graphic:** The sprite assigned to the creature. Seen only in the [premium version](Premium_version.md).
- **Tile:** The tile assigned to the creature, how you will see it without a graphic set.
- **Name:** The name of the creature as it displays in-game.
- **Playable:** If "No", the creature is not playable in any modes. "Fort" indicates that the creature is playable in fortress mode (`[SITE_CONTROLLABLE]`). "Adv" indicates that the creature is playable in adventure mode. All creatures except humans must have a population in an `[ALL_MAIN_POPS_CONTROLLABLE]` civilization in order to be playable in adventure mode; goblins (and other creatures) cannot be played from a goblin civ. Humans can be played whether or not a population exists due to `[OUTSIDER_CONTROLLABLE]`, but an `[ALL_MAIN_POPS_CONTROLLABLE]` civ still needs to have existed at some point. Creatures with `[LOCAL_POPS_CONTROLLABLE]` are also playable in adventure mode.
- **Hostile:** If "Yes", then the creature will attack on sight,<sup>\[[Verify](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=Talk:Creature&action=edit&redlink=1)\]</sup> if "No" then the creature is either neutral, or friendly - mindless [undead](Undead.md) creatures are always hostile to living things.
- **Food Source:** If "Yes", then the creature can be butchered into an [edible substance](Meat.md) that your dwarves will feed on.
- **Adult Body Size:** The average [size](Size.md) of the creature when an adult. This can be anywhere from 500 for a [rabbit](Rabbit.md), to 25,000,000 for a [dragon](Dragon.md). This value represents the creature's volume in cm<sup>3</sup>, which, for creatures made of flesh, more-or-less equals the creature's weight in grams.[\[1\]](http://www.bay12forums.com/smf/index.php?topic=30026.msg831163#msg831163) These sizes do not correspond to the sizes which trigger [pressure plates](Pressure_plate~2.md). Size is modified with height and broadness (i.e. incredibly skinny and short is below the average weight, while a fat and tall one is above it).
- **Pet Value:** This is the base value that the creature and its butchering products can be bought and sold for during [trading](Trading.md).
- **Biome:** Where the creature can be found.
- **Features:** Any special features the creature possesses, including things such as [causing a syndrome](Cave_blob.md), [breathing fire](Dragon.md), or [spinning webs](Giant_cave_spider.md).

Note: If you wish to view alternate ways of sorting creatures, such as sorting by biomes and location, or sorting domestic creatures by features, there is a new page found here: [Alternate creature sorting](Alternate_creature_sorting.md)

### Creatures

#### Civilized

##### Main races

These are intelligent creatures that form the dominant [civilized](Civilisation.md) races of the world. While most are part of society, many have turned to [banditry](Bandits.md).

| [![Dwarf sprite.png](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/4/46/Dwarf_sprite.png)](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Dwarf_sprite.png) | ☺ | [Dwarf](Dwarf.md) | Fort, Adv | No | No | 60,000 | Not tameable | [Mountain halls](Mountain_halls.md), [dwarf fortresses](Fortress.md), [hillocks](Hillock.md) | Trading race |

| [![Goblin sprite.png](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/9/90/Goblin_sprite.png)](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Goblin_sprite.png) | g | [Goblin](Goblin.md) | Adv | Usually<sup>1</sup> | No | 60,000 | Not tameable | [Dark fortresses](Dark_fortress.md) and [dark pits](Dark_pit.md) | Snatchers<sup>2</sup> |
| [![Kobold sprite.png](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/9/90/Kobold_sprite.png)](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Kobold_sprite.png) | k | [Kobold](Kobold.md) | Adv | Usually<sup>1</sup> | No | 20,000 | Not tameable | [Caves](Cave.md) | Skulking race |

> 1. Whether or not you are hostile with select civilized races depends on the history of your game world, and its length. Shorter histories mean less ongoing wars and general hostility, good for a newer player to learn the basics.
> 2. Snatchers try to snatch children of other civilizations. Snatched individuals become part of the Snatcher's civilization.

##### Underground Tribes

Intelligent [animal people](Animal_people.md) that form crude civilizations underground, but will not trade with you. They wield some weapons and can join adventurers. They can also perform [ambushes](Ambush.md) once the [caverns](Cavern.md) are reached, depending on which creatures are hostile.

#### Livestock and Domestic Animals

Creatures that have long been [domesticated](Domestic_animal.md), and either play a part in the [meat industry](Meat_industry.md), or are simply [pets](Pet.md) to keep dwarves company. Note: Except for [wagons](Wagon.md), domestic animals can be bought at embark, or requested from dwarven caravans. Animals of these types below that are caught in the wild with [cage traps](Cage_trap.md) can be tamed after only one session with an [animal trainer](Animal_trainer.md).

#### Beasts and Monsters

All kinds of monstrous creatures that roam the land and underground caverns, including: [semi-megabeasts](Semi-megabeast.md), [megabeasts](Megabeast.md), and randomly generated ones that can take any form. all very powerful and can easily be game-ending.

##### Procedurally generated

These creatures are procedurally generated, and different for every savefile. Their raws may be extracted from the world.dat file in uncompressed save folders. Their sprite will appear as the closest resemblance to their randomly generated appearance, including their colors and design, such as having wings, trunks, tusks, etc. The animated sprites below are just a few possible sprites without custom colors added.

#### Wild Animals

This section includes wild [animals](Animals.md), as well as their giant and humanoid counterparts. Wild animals are mostly found roaming the wilderness. Many of them are predators, while others are benign, and will not attack unless being attacked first. Some will be drawn to your stockpiles to steal [drink](Steals_drink.md), [food](Steals_food.md) or [something shiny](Steals_items.md). Some can be easily overcome, and yet others can be significant threats, like the dreaded [elephant](Elephant.md).

##### Agitation

Disruption of the environment in a [savage](Savage.md) biome, such as [woodcutting](Woodcutting.md) or [fishing](Fishing.md), may cause the appearance of "agitated" or "irritated" animals. Agitated animals will directly seek out and attack dwarves, instead of their normal behavior. Agitation rate and threshold can be adjusted in the [difficulty](Difficulty.md#wilderness-irritation) [settings](Settings.md). Agitation can be removed by [taming](Taming.md) animals captured in [cage traps](Cage_trap.md). With [DFHack](DFHack.md), it is possible to check current agitation status by executing the command `agitation-rebalance status`[\[2\]](https://docs.dfhack.org/en/stable/docs/tools/agitation-rebalance.html).

#### Extinct

Added in the [Dino Update](Release_information/53.15.md), [extinct](Extinct.md) creatures are a separate class of creatures, including their [animal person](Animal_person.md) variants. These creatures are further classified by the prehistoric time period they existed in: [Cambrian](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambrian), [Ordovician](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordovician), [Silurian](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silurian), [Devonian](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devonian), [Carboniferous](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carboniferous), [Permian](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permian), [Triassic](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triassic), [Jurassic](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurassic), [Cretaceous](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretaceous) and [Cenozic](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cenozic), though this era classification currently has no effect on how or where they appear. A [world generation option](World_generation.md#real-world-extinct-creatures) controls if and where they appear. If generated in a world, the animal people can be playable in adventurer mode; some of the animal people listed here are based on [extinct vermin](Vermin.md#extinct), which are listed on that page. Bogeymen can transform into extinct creatures, even if the player has them disabled completely in a world.

### [Night Creatures](Night_creature.md) / Other

#### Night creatures

These creatures are either vicious creatures that attack in the night, or are created through certain [interactions](Interaction_token.md) - be it a condition of the game, or intentionally by another creature.

> 1. In some worlds, intelligent experiments escape their creators and join normal civilizations. They will then be playable in adventurer mode.<br>
> 2. The player cannot normally start out as an intelligent undead, but can [unretire](Unretire.md) a former adventurer that has been resurrected. This can also be done (without unretiring) by using [adventurer parties](Adventurer_mode_character_creation.md#party-summary).<br>
> 3. The player cannot start out as a necromancer, but can gain necromancer powers by reading a slab or book containing the secrets of life and death.<br>
> 4. The player cannot start out as a vampire, but can become one by feeding on spilled vampire blood. [Animal people](Animal_people.md) with the ability to suck blood can also gain vampirism by blood-sucking a vampire during combat.
> 5. The player cannot start out as a werebeast, but can [become one](Werebeast.md#becoming-a-werebeast) by getting bitten by one, or cursed by a deity.

### Trivia

- [![](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/0/05/Placeholder_sprite.png)](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Placeholder_sprite.png)
  
  [https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Placeholder_sprite.png](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Placeholder_sprite.png)
  
  The debug creature.
  
  If the game does not recognize a creature and/or cannot find the [sprite associated for it](Graphics.md#creature-graphics), the sprite will default to a blue, round, blob-like face with stubby legs.
- Before the release of the Dino Update, some procedurally-generated creatures took the forms of extinct creatures before they were officially added to the game, which could be seen through their descriptions. In graphics mode, their appearance will be limited to the sprites dedicated to generated creatures. One example can be seen in [this screenshot](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/1/14/Df_predino_example.png).
- Many of the original [underground creatures](Cavern.md#creatures) were drawn by [Tarn](Toady_One.md) and [Zach](ThreeToe.md) Adams "in a couple of notebooks in our parents' living room one day, more than a decade ago, when we were trying to populate the [new-at-the-time](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/v0.31:Release_information#The_Underground) 3D [underground](Cavern.md)." ([Bay 12 Games](http://www.bay12games.com/dwarves/index.html#2024-06-25))<br>While most of these were implemented in [v0.31](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/v0.31:Release_information), some concepts were left on the cutting room floor.
  - [Page 1](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_01.jpg): [Bugbat](Bugbat.md) and [drunian](Drunian.md).
  - [Page 2](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_02.jpg): [Manera](Manera.md) and king cave worm.
  - [Page 3](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_03.jpg): [Molemarian](Molemarian.md) and mole rat [man](Animal_person.md#subterranean-animal-people).
  - [Page 4](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_04.jpg): Magma seal, magma walrus, and [jabberer](Jabberer.md).
  - [Page 5](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_05.jpg): [Pond grabber](Pond_grabber.md), [blind cave bear](Blind_cave_bear.md), [cave dragon](Cave_dragon.md), and [reacher](Reacher.md).
  - [Page 6](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_06.jpg): [Gorlak](Gorlak.md) and [cave fish man](Cave_fish_man.md).
  - [Page 7](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_07.jpg): [Floating guts](Floating_guts.md), [drunian](Drunian.md), and grabber.
  - [Page 8](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_08.jpg): [Creeping eye](Creeping_eye.md), [voracious cave crawler](Voracious_cave_crawler.md), and [blind cave ogre](Blind_cave_ogre.md).
  - [Page 9](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_09.jpg): [Cap hopper](Cap_hopper.md), [magma crab](Magma_crab.md), and [crundle](Crundle.md).
  - [Page 10](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_10.jpg): [Hungry head](Hungry_head.md), [flesh ball](Flesh_ball.md), and [elk bird](Elk_bird.md).
  - [Page 11](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_11.jpg): [Helmet snake](Helmet_snake.md), [green devourer](Green_devourer.md), and [rutherer](Rutherer.md).
  - [Page 12](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_12.jpg): [Creepy crawler](Creepy_crawler.md).
  - [Page 13](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_13.jpg): [Draltha](Draltha.md), [giant earthworm](Giant_earthworm.md), and [blood man](Blood_man.md).
  - [Page 14](https://bay12games.com/dwarves/notebook/notebook_14.jpg): Stilt plucker.
